He calls with trips (A♠J♠) and flush draws (K♣Q♣), and about two fifths of your range is ahead of those hands. He raises now and then, with trips (A♦J♦) and flush draws (5♣4♣).

   - You hold more of the strong hands: trips (A♠Q♠). He has fewer.

   - The bets are mostly trips (A♠K♠), pocket pairs under the top card (J♦J♠) and king high (K♣Q♥). The checks are mostly pocket pairs under the top card (K♣K♥), trips (A♠4♠) and king high (K♠Q♥).
        [Bets: trips 39%, pocket pairs under the top card 27%, king high 23%, low unpaired hands 8%. Checks: pocket pairs under the top card 47%, trips 26%, king high 19%, low unpaired hands 4%.]
   - When you check he mostly checks back, so a check usually sees the next card for nothing.
        [After your check he bets 23% of the time.]
   - What would change it. About a quarter of your bets do not need him to fold at all. If he folded about three times in ten instead of about four times in ten, about three quarters of your bets would be better checked. If he folded about five times in ten, almost all of your checks would be better bet.

RANGE: You check almost your whole range here.
   - Why so little betting: against an all-in he sometimes folds (pocket pairs under the top card (9♥9♠) and gutshots (5♠4♠)), less than a bet of that size needs to pay with nothing behind it. He calls with trips (A♠J♠) and flushes (7♣6♣), and about a quarter of your range is ahead of those hands.

   - When you check he bets about half the time, with trips (A♠J♠) and flushes (7♣6♣).
        [After your check he bets 50% of the time.]
   - What would change it. Take the strongest fifth of hands out of his range and you would bet about a quarter of yours instead of about a tenth: it is those hands of his that hold you back. Without your own strongest fifth you would hardly bet at all: the rest of the betting leans on those hands being there. It would be much the same if he could not bet when checked to or if he could not raise.
        [Solved again as it is, your range bets 12% of the time. Without his strongest fifth of hands: 23%. Without your own strongest fifth: 2%. If he could not bet when checked to: 8%. If he could not raise: 12%.]

   - He is betting trips (A♠J♠), low unpaired hands (7♦6♦) and full houses (A♦A♠).

   - Why you continue this often: the call needs 30%, and nearly all of your range has that against what he is betting. Fewer continue than have the price: a hand that just has it now does not keep it when he bets again, so the bottom of those folds.
        [97% of your range has 30% equity or more against his betting range. You continue with 56%: call 44%, raise 12%.]
   - He holds more of the strong hands here: trips (A♠J♠) and full houses (A♦A♠).

   - If this were the last bet, nearly all of your range would continue: everything with the price. You fold about as much as makes a bet with nothing break even for him: fold more and he could bet any two cards, fold less and his bluffs would lose.
        [You fold 44%. His bet breaks even as a pure bluff when you fold 43%.]
   - You fold pocket pairs under the top card (Q♣Q♥) and middle pair (K♠Q♥). You call with full houses (A♦A♠), pocket pairs under the top card (Q♣Q♠) and trips (A♦4♦). The raises are trips (A♠4♠) and full houses (K♣K♥).
        [Fold 44%, call 44%, raise 12%.]
   - What would change it. Nothing tried moves it: without his strongest fifth of hands or without your own strongest fifth, your range would fold about as often. It comes from how the two ranges meet this board as a whole.
        [Solved again as it is, your range folds 43% of the time. Without his strongest fifth of hands: 43%. Without your own strongest fifth: 43%.]

   - What would change it. About a quarter of your bets do not need him to fold at all. If he folded about three times in ten instead of about four times in ten, about three quarters of your bets would be better checked. If he folded about five times in ten, almost all of your checks would be better bet.
        [Counted hand by hand from the solution, for the 33% bet, keeping everything else he does as it is: he folds 36% now. 25% of the betting is worth more than checking even with no folds; 75% of it stops being so with ten points fewer folds. The street is too large to solve again here.]
